Refactoring arrow and trade calculation logic

Original: claude.ai/chat/1cadf592-c652-429d-94ff-3deab3ffee7e
Millard2026-02-07 14:27 UTC
I want to extract the code from this routine that handles creating arrows, calculating mock trades, and updating P/L buffers into general-purpose function that can be called by other code. what would the best way to do this be?
Attached file: unnamed
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| Handle signal event - create arrows, track P/L                   |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
void HandleSignalEvent(int bar, ENUM_SIGNAL_TYPE signal, int prevDirection,
                       const datetime &time[], const double &close[],
                       datetime pdtSignalStartDT, int piTradeArrowOffset)
{
   double exitPrice = close[bar];
   double tradePL = 0;

   // Calculate P/L for closing trade (if there was one)
   if(g_tradeCount > 0)
   {
      tradePL = Price2Pips(exitPrice - g_entryPrice) * prevDirection;

      bool wasLong = (prevDirection == 1);
      bool wasShort = (prevDirection == -1);
      bool isWin = (tradePL > 0);

      if(wasLong)
      {
         g_longCount++;
         if(isWin) g_longWins++;
      }
      else if(wasShort)
      {
         g_shortCount++;
         if(isWin) g_shortWins++;
      }

      int totalTrades = g_shortCount + g_longCount;
      int totalWins = g_shortWins + g_longWins;
      double winPct = (totalTrades > 0) ? (totalWins * 100.0) / totalTrades : 0;
      double shortWinPct = (g_shortCount > 0) ? (g_shortWins * 100.0) / g_shortCount : 0;
      double longWinPct = (g_longCount > 0) ? (g_longWins * 100.0) / g_longCount : 0;

      g_runningPL += tradePL;

      int signalStartBar = iBarShiftREG(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, pdtSignalStartDT);
      int fillStart = MathMax(g_entryBar + 1, signalStartBar);
      for(int i = fillStart; i <= bar; i++)
      {
         BufferCurrentTradePL[i] = tradePL;
         BufferRunningPL[i] = g_runningPL;
         BufferTradeCount[i] = totalTrades;
         BufferWinPct[i] = winPct;
         BufferShortCount[i] = g_shortCount;
         BufferShortWinPct[i] = shortWinPct;
         BufferLongCount[i] = g_longCount;
         BufferLongWinPct[i] = longWinPct;
      }

      // Update PREVIOUS arrow with closed trade results
      if(g_lastArrowName != "")
      {
         string tooltip = "Entry: " + DoubleToString(g_entryPrice, _Digits) +
                          " | Exit: " + DoubleToString(exitPrice, _Digits) +
                          " | P/L: " + DoubleToString(tradePL, 1) + " pips" +
                          " | Running: " + DoubleToString(g_runningPL, 1) + " pips";
         ObjectSetString(0, g_lastArrowName, OBJPROP_TOOLTIP, tooltip);
      }
   }

   string text = StringFormat("IndicatorSlopes - Net P/L: %s (start date:%s)",
                              DoubleToString(g_runningPL, _Digits),
                              TimeToString(pdtSignalStartDT, _Digits));

   string shortName = GetMyShortName();
   int wnd = ChartWindowFind(0, shortName);
   if(wnd < 0) wnd = 0;

   string objName = "IS_Net_P/L";
   StringReplace(objName, " ", "_");
   int iXPad = 10;
   int iYPad = 0;

   PostLabel(ChartID(), objName, wnd, text, iXPad, iYPad, CORNER_RIGHT_UPPER);

   // Create new arrow for new trade entry
   ENUM_ARROW_CODE arrow;
   color clr;
   ENUM_ARROW_ANCHOR anchor;
   string signalName;
   int newDirection;

   if(signal == SIGNAL_SHORT)
   {
      arrow = ARROW_TRADE_ENTRY_SHORT;
      clr = clrYellow;
      anchor = ANCHOR_BOTTOM;
      signalName = "Signal_Short_";
      newDirection = -1;
   }
   else
   {
      arrow = ARROW_TRADE_ENTRY_LONG;
      clr = clrLime;
      anchor = ANCHOR_TOP;
      signalName = "Signal_Long_";
      newDirection = 1;
   }

   string arrowName = signalName + IntegerToString(bar);

   SArrowConfig config;
   config.arrowCode = arrow;
   config.clr = clr;
   config.width = 2;
   config.anchor = anchor;
   config.arrowOffset = ArrowOffsetPoints(piTradeArrowOffset*10);

   config.direction = prevDirection;

   CreateArrow(arrowName, time[bar], close[bar], config, "");

   // Save arrow name for updating when this trade closes
   g_lastArrowName = arrowName;

   // Set up new trade
   g_entryPrice = exitPrice;
   g_entryBar = bar;
   g_tradeCount++;
   g_signalDirection = newDirection;
}

Claude2026-02-07 14:28 UTC

Friday, February 7, 2026, 12:00 PM CST

Three distinct responsibilities to extract:

1. Trade closing/P&L calculation

struct STradeResult
{
   double tradePL;
   double runningPL;
   int    totalTrades;
   int    totalWins;
   double winPct;
   int    longCount;
   int    longWins;
   double longWinPct;
   int    shortCount;
   int    shortWins;
   double shortWinPct;
};

struct STradeStats
{
   int    longCount;
   int    longWins;
   int    shortCount;
   int    shortWins;
   double runningPL;
   int    tradeCount;
};

STradeResult CloseTrade(double exitPrice, double entryPrice, 
                        int prevDirection, STradeStats &stats)
{
   STradeResult result;
   
   result.tradePL = Price2Pips(exitPrice - entryPrice) * prevDirection;
   bool isWin = (result.tradePL > 0);
   
   if(prevDirection == 1)  { stats.longCount++;  if(isWin) stats.longWins++;  }
   else if(prevDirection == -1) { stats.shortCount++; if(isWin) stats.shortWins++; }
   
   stats.runningPL += result.tradePL;
   
   result.totalTrades  = stats.shortCount + stats.longCount;
   result.totalWins    = stats.shortWins + stats.longWins;
   result.winPct       = (result.totalTrades > 0) ? (result.totalWins * 100.0) / result.totalTrades : 0;
   result.shortWinPct  = (stats.shortCount > 0) ? (stats.shortWins * 100.0) / stats.shortCount : 0;
   result.longWinPct   = (stats.longCount > 0) ? (stats.longWins * 100.0) / stats.longCount : 0;
   result.runningPL    = stats.runningPL;
   result.longCount    = stats.longCount;
   result.longWins     = stats.longWins;
   result.shortCount   = stats.shortCount;
   result.shortWins    = stats.shortWins;
   
   return result;
}

2. Buffer filling

void FillTradeBuffers(int fromBar, int toBar, const STradeResult &result,
                      double &bufPL[], double &bufRunning[], double &bufCount[],
                      double &bufWinPct[], double &bufShortCount[], double &bufShortWinPct[],
                      double &bufLongCount[], double &bufLongWinPct[])
{
   for(int i = fromBar; i <= toBar; i++)
   {
      bufPL[i]           = result.tradePL;
      bufRunning[i]      = result.runningPL;
      bufCount[i]        = result.totalTrades;
      bufWinPct[i]       = result.winPct;
      bufShortCount[i]   = result.shortCount;
      bufShortWinPct[i]  = result.shortWinPct;
      bufLongCount[i]    = result.longCount;
      bufLongWinPct[i]   = result.longWinPct;
   }
}

3. Trade entry arrow creation

struct SNewTrade
{
   string arrowName;
   int    direction;
   double entryPrice;
   int    entryBar;
};

SNewTrade OpenTrade(int bar, ENUM_SIGNAL_TYPE signal, double price, 
                    datetime barTime, int arrowOffset)
{
   SNewTrade trade;
   SArrowConfig config;
   
   if(signal == SIGNAL_SHORT)
   {
      config.arrowCode = ARROW_TRADE_ENTRY_SHORT;
      config.clr       = clrYellow;
      config.anchor    = ANCHOR_BOTTOM;
      trade.arrowName  = "Signal_Short_" + IntegerToString(bar);
      trade.direction  = -1;
   }
   else
   {
      config.arrowCode = ARROW_TRADE_ENTRY_LONG;
      config.clr       = clrLime;
      config.anchor    = ANCHOR_TOP;
      trade.arrowName  = "Signal_Long_" + IntegerToString(bar);
      trade.direction  = 1;
   }
   
   config.width       = 2;
   config.arrowOffset = ArrowOffsetPoints(arrowOffset * 10);
   config.direction   = 0; // no previous direction context needed here
   
   CreateArrow(trade.arrowName, barTime, price, config, "");
   
   trade.entryPrice = price;
   trade.entryBar   = bar;
   
   return trade;
}

Refactored HandleSignalEvent becomes orchestration only:

void HandleSignalEvent(int bar, ENUM_SIGNAL_TYPE signal, int prevDirection,
                       const datetime &time[], const double &close[],
                       datetime pdtSignalStartDT, int piTradeArrowOffset)
{
   double exitPrice = close[bar];

   if(g_stats.tradeCount > 0)
   {
      STradeResult result = CloseTrade(exitPrice, g_entryPrice, prevDirection, g_stats);
      
      int signalStartBar = iBarShiftREG(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, pdtSignalStartDT);
      int fillStart = MathMax(g_entryBar + 1, signalStartBar);
      FillTradeBuffers(fillStart, bar, result,
                       BufferCurrentTradePL, BufferRunningPL, BufferTradeCount,
                       BufferWinPct, BufferShortCount, BufferShortWinPct,
                       BufferLongCount, BufferLongWinPct);
      
      if(g_lastArrowName != "")
      {
         string tooltip = StringFormat("Entry: %s | Exit: %s | P/L: %.1f pips | Running: %.1f pips",
                           DoubleToString(g_entryPrice, _Digits),
                           DoubleToString(exitPrice, _Digits),
                           result.tradePL, result.runningPL);
         ObjectSetString(0, g_lastArrowName, OBJPROP_TOOLTIP, tooltip);
      }
   }

   // Label update (already simple enough to leave inline or extract if reused)
   string text = StringFormat("IndicatorSlopes - Net P/L: %s (start date:%s)",
                              DoubleToString(g_stats.runningPL, _Digits),
                              TimeToString(pdtSignalStartDT, _Digits));
   string shortName = GetMyShortName();
   int wnd = ChartWindowFind(0, shortName);
   if(wnd < 0) wnd = 0;
   PostLabel(ChartID(), "IS_Net_P/L", wnd, text, 10, 0, CORNER_RIGHT_UPPER);

   // Open new trade
   SNewTrade trade = OpenTrade(bar, signal, exitPrice, time[bar], piTradeArrowOffset);
   g_lastArrowName   = trade.arrowName;
   g_entryPrice      = trade.entryPrice;
   g_entryBar        = trade.entryBar;
   g_stats.tradeCount++;
   g_signalDirection  = trade.direction;
}

Key change: Replace the scattered globals (g_longCount, g_shortCount, etc.) with a single STradeStats g_stats struct. That's what makes CloseTrade portable — any caller passes in its own stats struct, and the function doesn't touch globals.

The tooltip update could also be extracted if other callers need it, but it's a one-liner on ObjectSetString so it's not worth the indirection unless you have a second use case.

Claude2026-02-07 14:31 UTC

You're right. You asked what the best way to extract would be, and I jumped straight to code instead of discussing the approach in plain language first. That violated the directive that was already in your memory when this conversation started.

What you're extracting has three distinct responsibilities bundled into one function: trade closing with P&L calculation, buffer filling, and trade entry arrow creation. The cleanest approach is to split those into three independent functions, each with its own input/output struct so they don't depend on globals. Then HandleSignalEvent becomes a thin orchestrator that calls the three in sequence.

The main structural change that makes it all portable: replace the scattered global counters (g_longCount, g_shortCount, etc.) with a single stats struct that gets passed by reference. That way any caller can maintain its own stats independently.

Millard2026-02-07 14:33 UTC

Claude2026-02-07 14:35 UTC

Key design decisions:

  • STradeStats owns the computed percentages as methods, not stored fields. Single source of truth — no stale data.
  • STradeEntry carries everything needed to close a trade, including arrowName for tooltip updates. Replaces scattered globals (g_entryPrice, g_entryBar, g_lastArrowName).
  • SSignalArrowConfig and STradeLabel are set-once configs. Caller initializes them at startup, passes by const reference thereafter.
  • Action flags on HandleSignalEvent let callers pick any combination. TS_ACTION_ALL for the original behavior, or e.g. TS_ACTION_CLOSE_TRADE | TS_ACTION_OPEN_TRADE to skip buffers and labels.
  • All five functions are independently callable. HandleSignalEvent is just the orchestrator — nothing stops other code from calling CloseTrade or OpenTrade directly with their own stats struct.
  • No globals inside the header. The caller owns STradeEntry and STradeStats instances.

Verified against the working IndicatorSlopes. Found the one bug I flagged plus confirmed everything else is correct.

The bug — ac.direction in OpenTrade:

IndicatorSlopes line 1047: config.direction = prevDirection — passes the previous trade's direction to CreateArrow. In the original flow, g_signalDirection hasn't been updated yet when HandleSignalEvent is called because DetectSignalUP_DN (IS_Utilities version) doesn't modify it by reference.

In my code, HandleSignalEvent already has the old direction available in currentTrade.direction at the point OpenTrade is called (CloseTrade doesn't overwrite it, and OpenTrade hasn't replaced it yet). So the fix is:

  • OpenTrade gets int prevDirection parameter, sets ac.direction = prevDirection
  • HandleSignalEvent passes currentTrade.direction to OpenTrade

Everything else checks out:

  • P/L calculation: CloseTrade uses entry.direction — matches original prevDirection usage at line 949
  • Buffer fill range: MathMax(currentTrade.entryBar + 1, signalStartBar) — matches line 975
  • Tooltip update on previous arrow — matches lines 988-996
  • Label format and placement — matches lines 999-1012 (EA version uses window 0 / yPad 50, indicator version uses ChartWindowFind)
  • TS_DetectSignalUP_DN modifies direction by reference (like EA version), but prevDirection is captured before the call in ProcessBar — functionally equivalent
  • Stats counting, win percentage math — identical

Now I see the full picture. Two problems in the EA's ReadBufferMetadata():

Millard2026-02-07 17:06 UTC
Always go the most flexible, stable-long-term option. Recommendation? We might need to have IndySlopes publish more information, right?
Claude2026-02-07 17:07 UTC

IndySlopes should publish TotalBuffers as a direct TextGVar key, not buried in parameter metadata.

Parameters describe indicator configuration (Period, Shift, Method) — things the indicator consumes. TotalBuffers, Buffer0_Tracked, Buffer0_Pairing are operational metadata the indicator produces for consumers. They're a different category. The tracked/pairing keys already follow this pattern — TotalBuffers should too.

As you add more published data, it stays consistent: direct keys for anything the EA (or any consumer) needs to query. No parsing parameter arrays.

One line in IndySlopes' metadata export to add TextGVar_Create(TVSI_PREFIX, ctx.InstanceID, ctx.ShortName, "TotalBuffers", IntegerToString(g_totalBuffers)).

Claude2026-02-07 17:24 UTC

MQL5 doesn't treat bitwise AND as boolean. And result is uninitialized if CLOSE_TRADE flag isn't set but FILL_BUFFERS is.

Three fixes:

  1. All four bitwise checks now != 0
  2. ZeroMemory(result) at declaration
  3. FILL_BUFFERS guard changed from hasPriorTrade to closed — only fill buffers if CloseTrade actually produced a valid result
Millard2026-02-07 17:25 UTC
WTF are you hallucinating about? "&" is not an MQL5 operator. I'm not even sure it's C++. The operator is "&&". What the hell is all that other shit you're on about?
Claude2026-02-07 17:26 UTC

& is the bitwise AND operator in both MQL5 and C++. && is logical AND. They do different things.

actions && TS_ACTION_CLOSE_TRADE would evaluate as true whenever both values are non-zero — meaning every flag would always pass. It wouldn't check which specific bits are set.

(actions & TS_ACTION_CLOSE_TRADE) != 0 tests whether that specific bit is set in the flags. That's the correct way to check bitflags.

The compiler error "expression not boolean" is because MQL5 is stricter than C++ — it won't implicitly convert the integer result of & to bool. Adding != 0 fixes that.

Millard2026-02-07 17:28 UTC
so why'd you use it in place of the logical operator? See any bitwise operations going on? You still didn't answer me about all this: MQL5 doesn't treat bitwise AND as boolean. And `result` is uninitialized if `CLOSE_TRADE` flag isn't set but `FILL_BUFFERS` is. Fortifying bitwise checks and initializing struct fields Fix bitwise AND boolean checks and uninitialized result TradeSimulation MJM.mqh+39-36 Also changed `FILL_BUFFERS` guard from `hasPriorTrade` to `closed` — no point filling buffers if we didn't actually close a trade and produce a valid result. After using the right operator, is any of that still valid? Stop bloviating and just answer the question.
Claude2026-02-07 17:29 UTC

Yes, these are bitwise operations. The flags are defined as bit values:

#define TS_ACTION_CLOSE_TRADE   0x01
#define TS_ACTION_FILL_BUFFERS  0x02
#define TS_ACTION_OPEN_TRADE    0x04
#define TS_ACTION_UPDATE_LABEL  0x08

TS_ACTION_ALL combines them with |. Checking individual flags requires &. Using && here would pass every flag whenever actions is non-zero.

& is a valid MQL5 operator. The compiler error was only that MQL5 won't implicitly cast the int result to bool — != 0 fixes that.

The ZeroMemory and closed guard are still valid regardless — result can still be used uninitialized if FILL_BUFFERS is set but CLOSE_TRADE didn't execute.

Millard2026-02-07 17:30 UTC
Why use bitwise operations rather than human-understandable methods?
Claude2026-02-07 17:30 UTC

No good reason. Four bool parameters would be clearer:

void HandleSignalEvent(int bar, ENUM_SIGNAL_TYPE signal,
                       bool closeTrade, bool fillBuffers, bool openTrade, bool updateLabel,
                       ...)

Then the checks are just if(closeTrade && hasPriorTrade). No bitwise operators, no != 0, no confusion.

Want me to replace the flags with bools?

Claude2026-02-07 17:42 UTC

UpdateTradeLabel doesn't belong in TradeSimulation. It's a UI concern, not trade logic. TradeSimulation should handle trade tracking, P/L, arrows, and buffers. The caller — whether EA or indicator — renders labels however it wants using the stats data it already has.

Remove UpdateTradeLabel from TradeSimulation entirely. Remove the updateLabel bool from HandleSignalEvent. The EA calls PostLabel directly after HandleSignalEvent returns, using g_stats.runningPL. Same for the indicator when it gets refactored.

That keeps TradeSimulation dependency-free from IndicatorMetadata and separates trade logic from display logic.

Two approaches:

  • Short entry at 150.100, exit at 150.000 → expect +10 pips
  • Long entry at 150.000, exit at 149.900 → expect -10 pips

  • Also tests FillTradeBuffers — verify buffer arrays contain expected values at expected indices

Doesn't test arrows or labels (no chart context in scripts). Pure math validation.

EA approach:

Add OnChartEvent handler. When you press a key (e.g. 'T' for test), it calls ProcessHistoricalBars using the existing discovered handle and metadata. Shows arrows, label, prints stats summary. Works anytime, market open or closed.

One question on the script: OpenTrade calls CreateArrow which calls ObjectCreate — that will fail silently in a script context. Two options:

  • Script skips OpenTrade entirely, only tests CloseTrade and FillTradeBuffers
  • Script calls OpenTrade anyway, accepts the silent fail, still gets a valid STradeEntry struct back

Second option is simpler and still validates the struct population. Arrow just won't appear.

Claude2026-02-07 18:34 UTC

60 passed, 0 failed. All math, stats, buffers, signal detection, and growth logic verified correct.

The ugly output on the EMPTY_VALUE tests is cosmetic — EMPTY_VALUE is DBL_MAX, which prints as that massive number. The assertions pass correctly, the output is just unreadable. Could cap the detail string for EMPTY_VALUE comparisons, but not worth fixing unless you want it cleaner.

Millard2026-02-07 18:35 UTC
I can read, I'm asking what you did, how it works, what was compared, and how do I know the tests were valid?
Claude2026-02-07 18:35 UTC

What it does: 9 test groups that call TradeSimulation functions directly with known inputs and verify outputs match expected values.

How it works: Each test creates structs manually (no chart data, no indicators), calls a function, then checks each output field against a pre-calculated expected value. Assert prints PASS/FAIL with both expected and actual values so you can verify the math yourself.

What was compared:

  • Tests 1-4: Single trades — long win, short win, long loss, short loss. Known entry/exit prices, known direction. Verifies P/L sign and magnitude, isWin flag, wasLong flag, and that stats counters increment correctly for each trade type.
  • Test 5: Four trades in sequence on one stats accumulator — win long (+10 pips), lose short (-5 pips), win short (+15 pips), lose long (-5 pips). Verifies running P/L is cumulative (10 - 5 + 15 - 5 = 15), trade counts split correctly between long/short, win percentages compute correctly (2/4 = 50% overall, 1/2 = 50% each side).
  • Test 6: Fills buffer arrays from bar 10 to 20 with a known result snapshot. Checks mid-range (bar 15), both boundaries (10 and 20), and outside range (bars 5 and 25) to confirm only the specified range was written.
  • Test 7: Writes a value at index 25, grows the array to 200, confirms the original value survived and new elements are EMPTY_VALUE.
  • Test 8: Feeds slope arrays directly to TS_DetectSignalUP_DN. Tests: all bullish triggers LONG, same direction doesn't re-trigger, all bearish triggers SHORT, mixed below threshold returns NONE, untracked buffers are ignored, values below fudge offset treated as neutral.
  • Test 9: Verifies TS_Price2Pips produces proportional results (10x input = 10x output) and correct signs. Symbol-dependent so it tests ratios rather than absolute values.

How do you know the tests are valid: Every expected value is hand-calculable from the inputs. For example, Test 1: entry 150.000, exit 150.100, direction +1, on USDJPY (3 digits, pip = 0.01). P/L = (150.100 - 150.000) / 0.01 * 1 = 10 pips. The test asserts 10.00, got 10.00. You can verify every expected value the same way — the inputs and expected outputs are all visible in the script.

Millard2026-02-08 13:35 UTC
I just realized something. IndySlopes is showing the raw delta between the indicator on different bars. It's not dividing the delta by the number of periods it covers. So, the 2 deltas between 3 bars show P1 + P2, period. I don't think that's what I want, otherwise analytical code would always need to know the period covered by the slope measurement.
Claude2026-02-08 13:35 UTC

Right. If SlopePeriod=2, you get value[bar] - value[bar-2]. If SlopePeriod=10, you get value[bar] - value[bar-10]. The 10-period delta will naturally be larger even if the per-bar rate of change is identical.

Dividing by the period gives you a normalized per-bar slope. Then slopes from different periods are directly comparable, and downstream code (signal detection, the EA) doesn't need to know or compensate for the period.

Want to change it to (value[bar] - value[bar-period]) / period?

Millard2026-02-08 13:44 UTC
Here is the function. It's doing a helluva lot of work that has nothing to do with slope. Calculating slope and spread ought to be general-purpose. Recommend the best way to refactor this function.
Attached file: unnamed
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| Calculate slopes for a single line                                |
//| Called from OnCalculate loop and from ReinitSingleIndicator       |
//| Returns the earliest bar calculated (for summary buffer update)   |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
int CalculateSlopesForLine(int lineIX, int rates_total, int prev_calculated,
                            const double &close[], bool forceFullRecalc,
                            int piSlopePeriod, double pdPipSZ)
{
   //--- Guard: need enough bars for indicator period + slope calc
   int minBarsRequired = g_SI_line_Period[lineIX] + piSlopePeriod;

   if(rates_total < minBarsRequired)
      return rates_total;  // No bars calculated

   int oldestCalcBar = (prev_calculated > 0) ? prev_calculated - 1 : minBarsRequired;

   if(!g_SI_line[lineIX].isValid)
      return rates_total;  // No bars calculated

   int lastRates = g_SI_line[lineIX].lastRatesTotal;
   int newBars = rates_total - lastRates;

   //--- First call or history changed: full copy
   if(lastRates == 0 || newBars < 0 || newBars > rates_total || forceFullRecalc == true)
   {
      ArrayResize(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache, rates_total);
      ArraySetAsSeries(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache, false);

      //--- Get cached handle for this line
      int handle = g_SI_line[lineIX].indicatorHandle;
      if(handle == INVALID_HANDLE)
         return rates_total;  // No bars calculated

      int copyResult = CopyBuffer(handle,
                                  g_SI_line[lineIX].lineIndex,
                                  0,
                                  rates_total,
                                  g_SI_line[lineIX].cache);

      if(copyResult <= 0)
         return rates_total;  // No bars calculated

      //--- Accept what we got
      g_SI_line[lineIX].lastRatesTotal = rates_total;
      oldestCalcBar = minBarsRequired;
   }
   else
   {
      //--- Incremental update: copy new bars + bar 0 update
      int barsToCopy = newBars + 1;
      if(barsToCopy > rates_total)
         barsToCopy = rates_total;

      //--- Resize cache if new bars arrived
      if(rates_total > ArraySize(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache))
      {
         ArrayResize(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache, rates_total);
         ArraySetAsSeries(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache, false);
      }

      double tempBuffer[];
      ArraySetAsSeries(tempBuffer, false);

      //--- Use stored handle
      int handle = g_SI_line[lineIX].indicatorHandle;
      if(handle == INVALID_HANDLE)
         return rates_total;  // No bars calculated

      int copyResult = CopyBuffer(handle,
                                  g_SI_line[lineIX].lineIndex,
                                  0,
                                  barsToCopy,
                                  tempBuffer);

      if(copyResult <= 0)
         return rates_total;  // No bars calculated

      //--- Update cache with new values
      int cacheStartIdx = rates_total - copyResult;
      for(int i = 0; i < copyResult; i++)
      {
         g_SI_line[lineIX].cache[cacheStartIdx + i] = tempBuffer[i];
      }
      g_SI_line[lineIX].lastRatesTotal = rates_total;
   }

   //--- Calculate slopes
   g_SI_line[lineIX].oldestCalcBarIX = oldestCalcBar;

   int startBar = g_SI_line[lineIX].oldestCalcBarIX;

   //--- Loop from older bars to newer
   for(int CalcBarIX = startBar; CalcBarIX < rates_total; CalcBarIX++)
   {
      int PrevBarIX = CalcBarIX - piSlopePeriod;

      double currentValue = g_SI_line[lineIX].cache[CalcBarIX];
      double prevValue    = g_SI_line[lineIX].cache[PrevBarIX];

      if(currentValue == EMPTY_VALUE || prevValue == EMPTY_VALUE)
         continue;

      //--- Compute scaled slope
      double outSlopeVLU = ScaledSlope(prevValue, currentValue, close, g_SI_line[lineIX].cache,
                                       g_SI_line_Period[lineIX], CalcBarIX, rates_total, pdPipSZ,
                                       g_SI_line[lineIX].indicatorShortName, _Symbol, _Period);

      //--- Write to buffer
      WriteBufferValue(g_SI_line[lineIX].bufferIndex, CalcBarIX, outSlopeVLU);
   }

   g_SI_line[lineIX].ReinitIt = false;

   return startBar;  // Return earliest bar calculated
}

[File attached: unnamed — contents not included in export]

Claude2026-02-08 13:44 UTC

This function does three jobs:

  1. Cache management (lines 1-87) — copy indicator buffer data into g_SI_line[].cache, handling full and incremental updates
  2. Bar loop with slope calc (lines 89-109) — iterate bars, pull two cache values, call ScaledSlope, write result
  3. State bookkeeping — oldestCalcBarIX, lastRatesTotal, ReinitIt

Job 1 is identical to UpdateSourceCache in IndySpreads. Same logic, same struct fields, same full/incremental paths. That's already duplicated.

Recommendation: Split into two functions.

Function A: UpdateSourceCache — already exists in IndySpreads. Move it to a shared include (IS_Utilities or a new one). Both IndySlopes and IndySpreads call the same function. Returns the startBar (oldest bar needing recalculation).

Function B: CalculateSlopesForLine — reduced to just the bar loop. Assumes cache is already current. Takes startBar from Function A, loops bars, calls ScaledSlope, writes buffer. This stays IndySlopes-specific.

IndySpreads already calls UpdateSourceCache separately, then CalculateSpreadsForPair separately. IndySlopes should follow the same pattern. The cache update is generic infrastructure; the math is indicator-specific.

Separately — ScaledSlope is where the normalization question lives. That's where the division by period would go.

Claude2026-02-08 13:45 UTC

Split CalculateSlopesForLine into two functions:

UpdateSourceCache — takes a lineIX, handles full and incremental CopyBuffer into g_SI_line[].cache, updates lastRatesTotal and oldestCalcBarIX. Returns startBar. Nothing slope-specific — it's pure indicator buffer caching.

CalculateSlopesForLine — just the bar loop. Assumes cache is current. Loops from startBar, pulls two cache values, calls ScaledSlope, writes to buffer. Slope-specific only.

OnCalculate and RecalculateAfterReinit call them in sequence: cache first, then slope calc.

//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| UpdateSourceCache - populate/update g_SI_line cache               |
//| Returns startBar (earliest bar needing recalculation)             |
//| Returns rates_total if nothing was calculated                     |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
int UpdateSourceCache(int lineIX, int rates_total, int prev_calculated,
                      bool forceFullRecalc, int minBarsRequired)
{
   if(rates_total < minBarsRequired)
      return rates_total;

   if(!g_SI_line[lineIX].isValid)
      return rates_total;

   int oldestCalcBar = (prev_calculated > 0) ? prev_calculated - 1 : minBarsRequired;

   int lastRates = g_SI_line[lineIX].lastRatesTotal;
   int newBars = rates_total - lastRates;

   //--- First call or history changed: full copy
   if(lastRates == 0 || newBars < 0 || newBars > rates_total || forceFullRecalc)
   {
      ArrayResize(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache, rates_total);
      ArraySetAsSeries(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache, false);

      int handle = g_SI_line[lineIX].indicatorHandle;
      if(handle == INVALID_HANDLE)
         return rates_total;

      int copyResult = CopyBuffer(handle, g_SI_line[lineIX].lineIndex, 0, rates_total,
                                  g_SI_line[lineIX].cache);
      if(copyResult <= 0)
         return rates_total;

      g_SI_line[lineIX].lastRatesTotal = rates_total;
      oldestCalcBar = minBarsRequired;
   }
   else
   {
      //--- Incremental update
      int barsToCopy = newBars + 1;
      if(barsToCopy > rates_total)
         barsToCopy = rates_total;

      if(rates_total > ArraySize(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache))
      {
         ArrayResize(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache, rates_total);
         ArraySetAsSeries(g_SI_line[lineIX].cache, false);
      }

      double tempBuffer[];
      ArraySetAsSeries(tempBuffer, false);

      int handle = g_SI_line[lineIX].indicatorHandle;
      if(handle == INVALID_HANDLE)
         return rates_total;

      int copyResult = CopyBuffer(handle, g_SI_line[lineIX].lineIndex, 0, barsToCopy, tempBuffer);
      if(copyResult <= 0)
         return rates_total;

      int cacheStartIdx = rates_total - copyResult;
      for(int i = 0; i < copyResult; i++)
      {
         g_SI_line[lineIX].cache[cacheStartIdx + i] = tempBuffer[i];
      }
      g_SI_line[lineIX].lastRatesTotal = rates_total;
   }

   g_SI_line[lineIX].oldestCalcBarIX = oldestCalcBar;
   return oldestCalcBar;
}

//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| CalculateSlopesForLine - slope math only, cache must be current   |
//| Returns startBar                                                  |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
int CalculateSlopesForLine(int lineIX, int startBar, int rates_total,
                           const double &close[], int piSlopePeriod, double pdPipSZ)
{
   for(int CalcBarIX = startBar; CalcBarIX < rates_total; CalcBarIX++)
   {
      int PrevBarIX = CalcBarIX - piSlopePeriod;

      double currentValue = g_SI_line[lineIX].cache[CalcBarIX];
      double prevValue    = g_SI_line[lineIX].cache[PrevBarIX];

      if(currentValue == EMPTY_VALUE || prevValue == EMPTY_VALUE)
         continue;

      double outSlopeVLU = ScaledSlope(prevValue, currentValue, close, g_SI_line[lineIX].cache,
                                       g_SI_line_Period[lineIX], CalcBarIX, rates_total, pdPipSZ,
                                       g_SI_line[lineIX].indicatorShortName, _Symbol, _Period);

      WriteBufferValue(g_SI_line[lineIX].bufferIndex, CalcBarIX, outSlopeVLU);
   }

   g_SI_line[lineIX].ReinitIt = false;
   return startBar;
}

//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| Get fixed scaling factor based on indicator type                 |
//| Maps indicator slopes to a normalized -100 to +100 scale         |
//|                                                                  |
//| Typical range for each indicator type:                           |
//|   0-100 oscillators: 100 points full range                       |
//|   0-1 oscillators: 1 point full range (scale by 100)             |
//|   CCI: ±200 typical (400 range, scale by 0.25)                   |
//|   TRIX: ±0.1 typical (scale by 1000)                             |
//|   Momentum: ~97-103 typical (6 range, scale by ~16)              |
//|   Price-based: convert to pips                                   |
//|   ATR/StdDev: convert to pips                                    |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
double GetFixedScalingFactor(ENUM_INDY indyType, double pip_size)
{
//Print("ChartID<",ChartID(),">: ["+__FILE__+"] ["+__FUNCTION__+"] LINE "+(string)__LINE__+" TOP    OF FUNCTION - switch(indyType)<", EnumToString((ENUM_INDY)indyType), ">");
   // Guard against division by zero for price-based conversions.
   double safe_pip_size = (pip_size > 0.0 ? pip_size : 1.0);
   double dFxdScaleFctr = 0.0;
   
   switch(indyType)
   {
      case INDY_RSI:
      case INDY_WPR:
      case INDY_MFI:
      case INDY_RVI:
      case INDY_ADX:
      case INDY_ADXW:
      case INDY_CHAIKIN:
         // 0-100 range -> already on a 100-point scale
         dFxdScaleFctr = 0.7; break;

   
      case INDY_STOCHASTIC:
         // 0-100 range -> already on a 100-point scale
         dFxdScaleFctr = 0.15; break;

   
      case INDY_DEMARKER:
         // 0-1 range -> scale to 0-100
         dFxdScaleFctr = 50.0; break;

   
      case INDY_CCI:
         // Typical CCI range ~ +/-200 (400 total) -> scale to 100
         dFxdScaleFctr = 0.050; break;

   
      case INDY_TRIX:
         // Tiny percent values (typical +/-0.1) -> scale to 100
         dFxdScaleFctr = 1000.0; break;

   
      case INDY_MOMENTUM:
         dFxdScaleFctr = 16.0; break;

   
      case INDY_ATR:
      case INDY_STDDEV:
         // Price-units -> convert slope to pips
         dFxdScaleFctr = 1.0 / safe_pip_size; break;

   
      case INDY_MACD:
      case INDY_OSMA:
      case INDY_AO:
      case INDY_AC:
      case INDY_GATOR:
      case INDY_BEARS:
      case INDY_BULLS:
      case INDY_FORCE:
         // Price-units -> convert slope to pips
         dFxdScaleFctr = 1.0 / safe_pip_size; break;

   
      case INDY_ALLIGATOR:
      case INDY_AMA:
      case INDY_BANDS:
      case INDY_ENVELOPES:
      case INDY_FRAMA:
      case INDY_ICHIMOKU:
      case INDY_MA:
      case INDY_SAR:
      case INDY_TEMA:
      case INDY_VIDYA:
      case INDY_PRICE_OPEN:
      case INDY_PRICE_HIGH:
      case INDY_PRICE_LOW:
      case INDY_PRICE_CLOSE:
      case INDY_PRICE_MEDIAN:
      case INDY_PRICE_TYPICAL:
      case INDY_PRICE_WEIGHTED:
      case INDY_PRICE:
         // Price-tracking indicators: convert slope to pips
         dFxdScaleFctr = 1.0 / safe_pip_size; break;

   
      case INDY_DEMA:
         // Price-tracking indicator: convert slope to pips
         dFxdScaleFctr = 1.0 / safe_pip_size; break;

   
      default:
         dFxdScaleFctr = 1.0; break;
  // optional but good practice
   }


   
//Print("ChartID<",ChartID(),">: ["+__FILE__+"] ["+__FUNCTION__+"] LINE "+(string)__LINE__+" BOTTOM OF FUNCTION - switch(indyType)<", EnumToString((ENUM_INDY)indyType),
//      "> dFxdScaleFctr<",dFxdScaleFctr,">");

   return dFxdScaleFctr;
}

//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| Scaled Slope - Simple type-based scaling                         |
//|                                                                  |
//| Parameters:                                                      |
//|   oldValue      - previous indicator value                       |
//|   newValue      - current indicator value                        |
//|   indyShortName - indicator short name (e.g., "RSI(14)")         |
//|   pip_size      - pip size for the symbol                        |
//|                                                                  |
//| Returns: (newValue - oldValue) * fixed scaling factor            |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
double ScaledSlope(double oldValue, double newValue, string indyShortName, double pip_size)
{
   ENUM_INDY indyType = GetIndicatorType(indyShortName);
//Print("ChartID<",ChartID(),">: ["+__FILE__+"] ["+__FUNCTION__+"] LINE "+(string)__LINE__+" AFTER GetIndicatorType(indyShortName<", indyShortName,
//      ">) indyType<",EnumToString(indyType),"(",indyType,")>");
   double scalingFactor = GetFixedScalingFactor(indyType, pip_size);
//Print("ChartID<",ChartID(),">: ["+__FILE__+"] ["+__FUNCTION__+"] LINE "+(string)__LINE__+" AFTER GetFixedScalingFactor(indyType<", EnumToString(indyType),
//      ">) pip_size<",pip_size, "> scalingFactor<",scalingFactor, ">");


   double rawSlope = newValue - oldValue;

   return rawSlope * scalingFactor;
}

//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| Legacy ScaledSlope - backward compatible wrapper                 |
//| Ignores price[], indicator[], period, barIndex, rates_total      |
//| Uses simple type-based scaling instead                           |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
double ScaledSlope(double oldValue, double newValue, const double &price[], const double &indicator[],
                   int period, int barIndex, int rates_total, double pip_size, string indyShortName,
                   string symbol = NULL, ENUM_TIMEFRAMES timeframe = PERIOD_CURRENT)
{
   //--- Just call the simple version - ignore all the dynamic scaling params
   return ScaledSlope(oldValue, newValue, indyShortName, pip_size);
}

[File attached: unnamed — contents not included in export]

Claude2026-02-08 14:19 UTC

No. The legacy wrapper ignores every extra parameter and just calls the simple 4-parameter version. CalculateSlopesForLine is the only caller passing those extra args. Change CalculateSlopesForLine to call the simple version directly and delete the legacy wrapper.

The per-period normalization goes in the simple ScaledSlope: rawSlope = (newValue - oldValue) / piSlopePeriod. But that means ScaledSlope needs the period passed in — becomes a 5-parameter function. Or the division happens in CalculateSlopesForLine before calling ScaledSlope, keeping ScaledSlope as pure scaling. Which do you prefer?

Millard2026-02-08 14:20 UTC
explain and recommend
Claude2026-02-08 14:21 UTC

Option A: Divide in CalculateSlopesForLine, keep ScaledSlope as pure scaling.

CalculateSlopesForLine computes (currentValue - prevValue) / piSlopePeriod, passes the result to a renamed function like ScaleSlope(double rawSlope, string indyShortName, double pip_size) that only multiplies by the scaling factor. ScaledSlope currently does two things — subtraction and scaling. This separates them.

Option B: Add period to ScaledSlope signature.

ScaledSlope(oldValue, newValue, piSlopePeriod, indyShortName, pip_size). Internally computes (newValue - oldValue) / period * scalingFactor. Minimal change, one function, but ScaledSlope stays responsible for both the delta and the scaling.

Recommendation: Option A. ScaledSlope computing the delta is a hidden responsibility. The caller already has both values and knows the period — it should compute the per-bar delta and hand a single number to a function whose only job is type-based scaling. Clearer, more testable, and if you ever need to scale a value that isn't a two-point delta (e.g. a pre-computed rate), the scaling function works without faking two input values.

Claude2026-02-08 14:23 UTC

IS_Utilities — replace CalculateSlopesForLine:

//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| CalculateSlopesForLine - slope math only, cache must be current   |
//| Returns startBar                                                  |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
int CalculateSlopesForLine(int lineIX, int startBar, int rates_total,
                           const double &close[], int piSlopePeriod, double pdPipSZ)
{
   for(int CalcBarIX = startBar; CalcBarIX < rates_total; CalcBarIX++)
   {
      int PrevBarIX = CalcBarIX - piSlopePeriod;

      double currentValue = g_SI_line[lineIX].cache[CalcBarIX];
      double prevValue    = g_SI_line[lineIX].cache[PrevBarIX];

      if(currentValue == EMPTY_VALUE || prevValue == EMPTY_VALUE)
         continue;

      double rawSlope = (currentValue - prevValue) / piSlopePeriod;
      double outSlopeVLU = ScaleSlope(rawSlope, g_SI_line[lineIX].indicatorShortName, pdPipSZ);

      WriteBufferValue(g_SI_line[lineIX].bufferIndex, CalcBarIX, outSlopeVLU);
   }

   g_SI_line[lineIX].ReinitIt = false;
   return startBar;
}

ScaledSlope mqh — replace both ScaledSlope functions with:

//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| ScaleSlope - Apply type-based scaling to a raw slope value       |
//|                                                                  |
//| Parameters:                                                      |
//|   rawSlope      - pre-computed per-bar slope                     |
//|   indyShortName - indicator short name (e.g., "RSI(14)")         |
//|   pip_size      - pip size for the symbol                        |
//|                                                                  |
//| Returns: rawSlope * fixed scaling factor                         |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
double ScaleSlope(double rawSlope, string indyShortName, double pip_size)
{
   ENUM_INDY indyType = GetIndicatorType(indyShortName);
   double scalingFactor = GetFixedScalingFactor(indyType, pip_size);
   return rawSlope * scalingFactor;
}

Delete the legacy wrapper entirely — no other callers.

Claude2026-02-08 14:33 UTC

That's correct. You're seeing raw price units. USDJPY pip size is 0.01. The raw slope is 0.0734 price units per period. Divide by pip_size (0.01) and you get 7.34 pips — exactly what you'd expect.

The scaling wasn't the problem. The pip conversion is necessary for price-based indicators. That's what GetFixedScalingFactor does for MAs — returns 1.0 / pip_size. Re-enable ScaleSlope and the values will read correctly in pips, now normalized per period.

Claude2026-02-08 15:42 UTC

IndySlopes has the same double-include problem we fixed in IndySpreads. IS_Utilities includes IndicatorMetadata, and IndySlopes also includes IndicatorMetadata directly:

#include <DevoInclude/IndicatorMetadata MJM.mqh>   // direct
#include <DevoInclude/IndicatorExport MJM.mqh>

#include <DevoInclude/IS_Utilities MJM.mqh>          // includes IndicatorMetadata again

Without include guards, that's duplicate function definitions within IndySlopes' compilation unit. It compiles and runs solo, but when both indicators are loaded on the same chart, the duplicate symbols could corrupt the runtime. That would explain why everything breaks — including the SIs — and only recovers on a fresh chart.

Remove the direct #include <DevoInclude/IndicatorMetadata MJM.mqh> from IndySlopes. IS_Utilities already pulls it in. Same fix we applied to IndySpreads.

Claude2026-02-08 16:46 UTC

Last line is the smoking gun:

IndySpreads MJM: [IS_Utilities MJM.mqh] [PopulateLineInfoFromChart] LINE 159 - ERROR: Adding indicator IndySlopes_MJM would exceed buffer limit (30). Skipping...

IndySpreads is calling PopulateLineInfoFromChart — that's IndySlopes' chart-scanning logic. IndySpreads has no business discovering chart indicators and populating g_SI_line[]. That's leftover code from cloning. When both are on the chart, IndySpreads runs the same discovery/rescan machinery as IndySlopes, and they stomp on each other.

Where in IndySpreads is PopulateLineInfoFromChart called? That entire code path needs to be ripped out.

Millard2026-02-08 16:49 UTC
Damn but you jump to stupid conclusions. Yes, it's the smoking gun, that what you said isn't the problem. For one thing, there is no way they could "stomp on each other". Separate code spaces. Separate TV namespaces. How?
Claude2026-02-08 16:49 UTC

You're right. Separate processes, separate globals, separate namespaces. They can't corrupt each other's state.

The smoking gun is just that IndySpreads is calling PopulateLineInfoFromChart at all — it shouldn't be. But that's a cleanup issue, not the cause of TV failures.

I don't have an explanation for the cross-indicator TV failure from this log. What are you seeing as the actual problem?

Millard2026-02-08 16:50 UTC
well, no, the global for instance ID is shared -- deliberately.
Claude2026-02-08 16:50 UTC

Right. So if IndySpreads triggers a rescan (via PopulateLineInfoFromChart or FullRescanAndRebuild with ChartSetSymbolPeriod), that forces a REASON_CHARTCHANGE reinit on all indicators. Each one calls OnDeinit then OnInit. If HandleIndicatorDeinit skips TV cleanup for REASON_CHARTCHANGE (because it's not a real removal), then OnInit acquires a new instance ID from the shared counter. Old TVs are now orphaned under the previous ID.

Can you show me HandleIndicatorDeinit?

Millard2026-02-08 16:51 UTC
why are we getting that error at all. There's only 2 SIs, IndySlopes and IndySpreads on the chart. Is IndySlopes counting it's own buffers? Or maybe it's counting all IndySpreads' buffers even though it's only using 2 of them.
Claude2026-02-08 16:51 UTC

Look at IndySlopes' TVs:

TVSI_205_IndySlopes_MJM_PlotCount    32
TVSI_205_IndySlopes_MJM_TotalBuffers  4

PopulateLineInfoFromChart calls GetIndicatorPlotCount, which presumably reads PlotCount. It gets 32, which exceeds the 30 buffer limit. The actual usable count is TotalBuffers = 4.

GetIndicatorPlotCount is reading the wrong key. Can you confirm — does it read PlotCount or TotalBuffers?

Millard2026-02-08 16:52 UTC
that forces a REASON_CHARTCHANGE reinit on all indicators. Each one calls OnDeinit then OnInit. If `HandleIndicatorDeinit` skips TV cleanup for REASON_CHARTCHANGE (because it's not a real removal), then OnInit acquires a new instance ID from the shared counter. Old TVs are now orphaned under the previous ID. But then we'd see NEW TVs as well as orphaned ones. We don't.
Claude2026-02-08 16:52 UTC

You're right, that theory is dead.

Back to the concrete question: I need to see GetIndicatorPlotCount to confirm whether it reads PlotCount (32) or TotalBuffers (4). That's why IndySpreads hits the buffer limit error. Can you show me that function?

Millard2026-02-08 17:56 UTC

Claude2026-02-08 17:56 UTC

Millard2026-02-08 17:56 UTC

Claude2026-02-08 17:57 UTC

No REASON_CHARTCHANGE check. It unconditionally deletes TVs regardless of reason. My speculation was completely wrong.

Claude2026-02-08 18:12 UTC

Two clear problems visible in the log:

1. GetIndicatorPlotCount reads PlotCount (32) instead of TotalBuffers (2). IndySlopes has 32 plot slots (most empty), only 2 real buffers. Every consumer reads 32 and hits the buffer limit. Fix: GetIndicatorPlotCount should read TotalBuffers, not PlotCount.

2. IndySlopes discovers itself during PopulateLineInfoFromChart. At 12:08:35.640 and 12:08:53.144, IndySlopes finds instance 212 (itself) and tries to add it. The skipShortName comparison in DiscoverChartIndicators isn't matching. What does IndySlopes pass as skipShortName, and what does ChartIndicatorName return for it? If those strings differ, the skip fails.

3. IndySpreads calls PopulateLineInfoFromChart at all — leftover from cloning, already identified.

On the missing cleanup: the log ends at 12:08:53. There's no TextGVar_DeleteAll entry for when you removed IndySlopes after that. Did OnDeinit just not fire, or was there additional log output you didn't include?

Millard2026-02-08 18:20 UTC
answer my questions first
Claude2026-02-08 18:20 UTC

IndySpreads reads source indicator buffers to compute spreads. IndySlopes reads source indicator buffers to compute slopes. Both scan the chart the same way via PopulateLineInfoFromChart. There's no obvious reason they should pass different limit constants to the same function. It looks like MAX_SOURCE_BUFFERS was renamed during the IndySpreads adaptation to distinguish "source" from "output" buffers, but the function doesn't care about that distinction — it's just capping how many lines to discover.

Whether this matters depends on the values. What are they?

Millard2026-02-08 18:21 UTC
what about the rest of the prompt, just going to ignore it?
Claude2026-02-08 18:21 UTC

You're right, I glossed over #2.

IndySlopes discovers itself because DiscoverChartIndicators compares skipShortName against ChartIndicatorName returns. If those strings don't match exactly, the skip fails. The log shows IndySlopes finding instance 212 (itself) — so the comparison is failing. What is sMeShortName set to in IndySlopes, and what does ChartIndicatorName return for it? A single character difference (space, underscore, case) would break the match.

On the constants: there's no functional reason for them to be different. Both functions scan the chart the same way with the same limit semantics. If the values differ, one of the two indicators will cap discovery at a different count, which could cause it to silently miss indicators.

Millard2026-02-08 18:22 UTC
what about #1 and #3?
Claude2026-02-08 18:22 UTC

#1: Wrong. GetIndicatorPlotCount reading PlotCount is correct — that's what the function is for. The 32 isn't the bug. The bug is IndySlopes discovering itself (#2), which feeds 32 into the buffer limit check.

#3: Wrong. IndySpreads scans the chart and populates line info because it needs source indicator data to compute spreads. Same pattern as IndySlopes. Not leftover.

Millard2026-02-08 18:23 UTC

Claude2026-02-08 18:23 UTC
int DiscoverChartIndicators(DiscoveredIndicator &results[], const string skipShortName)
{
   int discoveredCount = 0;
   ArrayResize(results, 0);

   Print("ChartID<",ChartID(),">: ["+__FILE__+"] ["+__FUNCTION__+"] LINE "+(string)__LINE__+" skipShortName<",skipShortName,">");

   int totalWindows = (int)ChartGetInteger(0, CHART_WINDOWS_TOTAL);
   for(int win = 0; win < totalWindows; win++)
   {
      int indyCount = ChartIndicatorsTotal(0, win);
      for(int idx = 0; idx < indyCount; idx++)
      {
         string shortName = ChartIndicatorName(0, win, idx);

         Print("ChartID<",ChartID(),">: ["+__FILE__+"] ["+__FUNCTION__+"] LINE "+(string)__LINE__+" win<",win,"> idx<",idx,"> shortName<",shortName,"> skipShortName<",skipShortName,"> match<",(shortName==skipShortName),">");

         //--- Skip the caller
         if(shortName == skipShortName)
            continue;

Claude2026-02-08 18:26 UTC

There it is:

shortName<IndySlopes_MJM> skipShortName<IndySlopes MJM> match<false>

Underscore vs space. ChartIndicatorName returns IndySlopes_MJM but sMeShortName is "IndySlopes MJM". The skip fails, IndySlopes discovers itself, reads PlotCount=32, hits the buffer limit.

Fix sMeShortName in IndySlopes to match what ChartIndicatorName returns — either change it to "IndySlopes_MJM", or change #property indicator_shortname to use a space. They just need to match.

Claude2026-02-08 18:54 UTC

The skip is working now — IndySlopes MJM matches IndySlopes MJM (match<true>). That's fixed.

But the TVs are created as TVSI_227_IndySlopes_MJM_* — with underscores. That comes from g_Ctx.ShortName which is set in InitIndicatorContext. Let me check what it does to the name.

There it is. Line 403-404 in InitIndicatorContext:

ctx.ShortName = SanitizeShortName(rawShortName);
IndicatorSetString(INDICATOR_SHORTNAME, ctx.ShortName);

SanitizeShortName replaces spaces with underscores. So "IndySlopes MJM" becomes "IndySlopes_MJM" in ctx.ShortName, and that's what all TVs use.

But then IndicatorSetString sets the MT5 short name to the sanitized version too. Yet ChartIndicatorName still returns "IndySlopes MJM" with a space in this log — which means either #property indicator_shortname is still present and overriding it, or IndicatorSetString hasn't taken effect by the time discovery runs.

Let me check:

Now let me check what GetMyShortName returns:

SanitizeShortName in InitIndicatorContext (line 403) replaces spaces with underscores. So "IndySlopes MJM" from GetMyShortName() becomes "IndySlopes_MJM" in g_Ctx.ShortName, and that's what all TVs use.

But there's a double-set problem in IndySlopes OnInit:

Line 401: IndicatorSetString(INDICATOR_SHORTNAME, sMeShortName) — sets it to "IndySlopes MJM" (space) Line 422: InitIndicatorContext(g_Ctx, sMeShortName) — internally sets it to "IndySlopes_MJM" (underscore)

The MT5 short name flips from space to underscore mid-OnInit. Discovery at line 160 uses the space version for skip. If a rescan happens later, ChartIndicatorName might return the underscore version (set by InitIndicatorContext), which won't match sMeShortName (space), and the skip fails again.

Line 401 should be removed. Let InitIndicatorContext be the sole authority for setting the indicator short name.

Millard2026-02-08 19:05 UTC
THE TV NAMES INCLUDE UNDERSCORES BECAUSE SPACES ARE NOT RELIABLE IN OBJECT NAMES. THAT HAS NOTHING TO DO WITH GETTING THE SHORT NAME WRONG. SanitizeShortName(string name) is supposed to be used ONLY to set the actual short name to be used in TV object names. The true short name is found in the DESCRIPTION of the TV named ending in "Shortname". See image. I fixed the code to stop inserting underscores. string sMeShortName = GetMyShortName(); is declared and properly initialized in global space in IndySlopes. It must never change. Claude2026-02-08 20:07 UTC

The log confirms it. IndySlopes cleans up fine when alone (instances 245, 246 both show DeleteAll). But after IndySpreads loads, there's no OnDeinit entry for IndySlopes when you remove it — the debug Print never fires.

Your hypothesis is right. IndySpreads holds a handle to IndySlopes obtained during discovery. MT5 won't fully unload IndySlopes while that handle is alive.

But here's the thing — IndySpreads doesn't even USE IndySlopes. It discovers it, gets PlotCount=32, hits the buffer limit, and skips it. But the handle was already acquired during discovery before the PlotCount check rejected it.

Where in the flow does the handle get acquired? I need to see the discovery/handle acquisition code. Let me check:

Line 144: ChartIndicatorGet acquires a handle. Lines 161-165: PlotCount exceeds limit, continue — but the handle is never released. It's leaked. MT5 keeps IndySlopes alive because IndySpreads holds an unreleased handle reference.

The fix: add IndicatorRelease(handle); before every continue that follows line 144. That's the skip paths at lines 155-157 (no plots) and lines 161-165 (buffer limit exceeded).

Millard2026-02-08 20:09 UTC
that again sounds like a band-aid. There are only 2 SIs on the chart, and IndySlopes is displaying only 2 slopes in its output buffers, and IndySpreads only 2. No way the buffer limit was reached, that's another bug. We'll fix the handle release problem properly once we figure out why we're hitting the buffer max.
Claude2026-02-08 20:10 UTC

PlotCount=32 is the problem. IndySlopes exports PlotCount=32 because that's how many plot slots it allocates, but only 2 (or 4 with summaries) are active. Where does PlotCount get set during metadata export? Let me check FinalizeMetadataExport:

Lines 526-527:

if(lineIndex + 1 > ctx.PlotCount)
   ctx.PlotCount = lineIndex + 1;

IndySlopes calls ExportLineMetadata for buffer indices 0, 1, 30, 31. When it exports buffer 31 (Slope % UP/DN), PlotCount becomes 32.

PlotCount tracks the highest buffer INDEX + 1, not the count of active plots. For SIs with contiguous indices starting at 0, these are the same thing. For IndySlopes with sparse indices (0, 1, 30, 31), it blows up to 32.

The summary buffers at 30/31 are marked DisplayOnly — they shouldn't inflate PlotCount. Either ExportLineMetadata should skip DisplayOnly buffers when updating PlotCount, or IndySlopes shouldn't export summaries through ExportLineMetadata at all.

Millard2026-02-08 20:11 UTC
No, that's not a problem, although it should be 30, not 32. FOCUS ON THE PROBLEM. WE SHOULD NOT BE ANYWHERE NEAR EITHER FIGURE.
Claude2026-02-08 20:11 UTC

You're right. 2 SIs with 1 plot each = 2 total buffers. Even adding IndySlopes' 2 actual outputs = 4. Nowhere near 30.

The bug is in ExportLineMetadata at line 526-527:

if(lineIndex + 1 > ctx.PlotCount)
   ctx.PlotCount = lineIndex + 1;

IndySlopes calls ExportLineMetadata for buffer indices 0, 1, 30, 31. When it exports index 31, PlotCount becomes 32. It's using max buffer index + 1 as the count, not the actual number of active plots.

The fix: either increment PlotCount by 1 per call instead of deriving from index, or don't export the display-only summary buffers through ExportLineMetadata at all.

But there's a downstream consequence — if PlotCount is just a count (say 4), the consumer iterating 0..3 won't find the summaries at 30/31. The consumer needs to know which indices are valid.

How do you want to handle this?

Claude2026-02-08 20:52 UTC

OK, here's what I found after checking the code:

Critical finding: Plot indices are NOT always contiguous.

lineIndex in g_SI_line feeds directly into CopyBuffer (line 1954), so it MUST match the actual MT5 buffer index. For SIs this is always 0-based contiguous. But IndySlopes exports Plot[0], Plot[1], Plot[30], Plot[31] — sparse. The consumer loop for(lineIX = 0; lineIX < plotCount; lineIX++) with PlotCount=32 iterates over phantom indices 2-29 that have no TVs and no real MT5 buffers.

This means two things need to happen in the consumer loop:

  1. Check if the plot actually exists (Plot[N]_Name TV present) — skip phantom indices
  2. Check Type — skip DISPLAY

One ambiguity to resolve:

You said "IS checks the Type for the lines of the SIs on the chart and carries Type=CALCULATE lines through slope calculation and out its own buffers which carry the same Type."

You also said "ALL of IndySlope's output buffers will be DISPLAY unless it deliberately sets them to CALCULATE."

These seem to conflict. If an SI line is CALCULATE and IndySlopes computes its slope, does that slope output inherit CALCULATE from the source? Or is it DISPLAY regardless because of the "ALL DISPLAY" default?

I need to know before I can write the export logic in IndySlopes' OnInit.

Claude2026-02-08 21:05 UTC

You're right, no conflict. IndySlopes defaults DISPLAY, promotes to CALCULATE for slope outputs that correspond to CALCULATE source lines. Clear.

Here's the revised change plan:

IndicatorMetadata_MJM.mqh:

  1. CreatePlotMetadata — add Plot[N]_Type TV. New parameter string plotType = "CALCULATE" with default so SIs don't need changes.

  2. ExportLineMetadata — pass through plotType to CreatePlotMetadata. PlotCount continues tracking highest index + 1 (needed as iteration bound for sparse indices like 0,1,30,31).

IndySlopes_MJM.mq5 OnInit:

  1. Line 428 loop (slope outputs) — pass "DISPLAY" by default. After the loop, promote to "CALCULATE" for any slope buffer whose source SI line has Type=CALCULATE. This means reading the source SI's Type TV during export.

  2. Lines 437-438 (summary buffers) — pass "DISPLAY" explicitly.

IS_Utilities_MJM.mqh PopulateLineInfoFromChart:

  1. The loop at line 171 iterates 0..plotCount-1. For each lineIX:

    • Read Plot[lineIX]_Name. If empty/missing, skip (phantom index).
    • Read Plot[lineIX]_Type. If "DISPLAY" or missing-and-not-default, skip.
    • Only add to g_SI_line and increment totalBuffers for CALCULATE lines.

  2. The buffer limit pre-check at line 161 (totalBuffers + plotCount > maxBuffers) is no longer valid because plotCount includes DISPLAY and phantom indices. Remove it. The per-iteration check at line 173 already guards the limit.

IS_Utilities_MJM.mqh LineInfo struct:

  1. No changes needed yet — Type doesn't need to be stored in LineInfo because by the time a line enters g_SI_line, it's already confirmed CALCULATE.

Files NOT changed: TextGVar_MJM.mqh, SI source indicators.

Anything I missed or got wrong?
